Get The Look: Shoes By Monique LHuillier, Top By Finders Keepers (similar top available here),  Skirt by Cmeo Collective (similar style available here), Bag By Jimmy Choo, Watch By Tory Burch xx,    A weekend getaway should be relaxing, evoke a sense of wanderlust and most importantly, be easy . And so should the packing experience: you need lightweight, non-crease, versatile pieces that can be layered, dressed up, and dressed down. Thankfully, two trends have re-emerged that make daily dressing (and travel style) a breeze. The 90’s are back, so channel your inner Kelly Kapowski (she was my absolute girl crush on Saved by the Bell!) and slip into a stretchy jersey dress – a knit or ribbed style adds an extra dose comfort, and is more flattering on your figure. I recommend stocking up on a few styles, as they are completely transitional season to season with a trench or oversized cardigan in the fall, and can be worn casually (with sneakers) or for a night out with open toe heels. Satisfy the second fashion trend, minimalism, by sticking to neutral tones. Mottled grey, black or white work best for a proper 90’s vibe). As you pack, put together a list of essential items that fit in a carry on and can be worn different ways. The perfect white tank, strappy black heels, a black silk camisole, fitted jeans, and a non-crease dress or romper aren’t just style investment pieces, they’re easy to pack essentials. Don’t forget to pack your beauty faves too – this is an area i won’t skimp on, because your skin still needs all its products. Just invest in travel sized bottles and pots for easy transportation. And don’t forget your makeup: with this particular look – red lip or cat eye would totally transform and modernize it from day to dinner. My Mum drilled that advice into me since I was a kid – and it really helps when you shop! Every closet needs a balance of chic investment pieces that will last you a lifetime, and a sprinkle of bright and bold items that show off this season’s cutest trends. It’s a big ying and ylang. Recently (perhaps after all the traveling), I’ve noticed that my style has transitioned to loving elegant, timeless pieces that I can style up and down with something bright or right off the runway. There’s something really satisfying about carefully curating capsule wardrobe: about 8 pieces that you’ll wear over and over. They need to be good quality, a silhouette that really suits you, and a neutral color. The first piece on my list, is this White House Black Market cashmere vest that I drooled over in their lookbook, and decided to pair with a silk blouse here for an important meeting, and later switched into ripped jeans and caged heels for dinner. This vest owes much of it’s versatility to it’s dove grey color scheme (this shade is going to huge for fall, so stock up), and looks best when you wear it worn. The key here is varying the palette with different shades of grey (excuse the pun!!) and contrasting luxe textures like silk, suede, denim and cashmere.  Credits: photographed by Samantha Marquart, sponsored by St. John Knits. I know it’s still summer, but it’s time to start spotting a few key pieces to add to your for fall/winter wardrobe. It’s always good to plan ahead with fashion, and get your styling juices flowing early! Layers truly make the outfit. And I always say that a great coat is a layering essential – your coat should always make a statement, it’s the first (and often only) thing people see in the winter – so make that first impression count. Most people opt for a simple black coat in the effort to keep styling easy, but I personally prefer navy. There’s something chic and European about a navy coat, and this season’s twist is to add a little print. Navy has become one of my palette obsessions – replacing black as a chic alternative that goes with everything from brights and metallics, to classic neutrals. This printed coat by St. John is a great investment piece as you can style it up or down with simple accessory alternates. It’s thin enough to wear while the weather’s still mild, and I love the soft, unstructured feel when I belt it. Kind of like a mix between an overcoat and a cardigan. Play around with your look this fall: switching the belt for something thicker can add a more formal touch and an hourglass silhouette. Adding a statement collar necklace and open toe sandals amp up the glam factor for an evening out. Or rock a thin tee and navy St. John leather pants, like I did, for a dressy day look. And keep your makeup dewy and natural – let the coat be the star of the show. GlamApp gave me apricot cheeks with a hinkt of shimmer on the brow bone and cheek bones, and a simple bend in the hair, leaving the ends straight for the messy vibe I love so much.               Get The Look: Coat and pants by St. John, Shoes by Jimmy Choo, Sunglasses by MinkPink (similar style here), Bracelet by Monica Vinader, Rings by Elizabeth and James xx,  P.S. Onesies are the perfect piece for easy, fun summer dressing. They’re are a full outfit in one, simple as that! But I love the fact that they come in short, long, loose and tight versions. They’re a bit more tomboy than a dress, but I often rock mine with heels to glam them up a little. Depending on the print and style, onesies can be anything from casual to dressy – both relying heavily on the right accessories and shoes. This romper has great texture and a beautiful persimmon color, so i kept the rest of my color palette one note: warm camel. The tall gladiator sandals are a big statement this season, and really means the rest of your outfit should remain pretty simple. I didn’t go for crazy jewelry or shades because it would have been too much. Pro tip: when wearing strappy heels or sandals, don’t skimp on the SPF – this pair was begging to give me funky tan lines which I avoided by reapplying my SPF 30 throughout the day.   xx,  Quite a few of you have asked about my fitness habits recently, and it got me thinking about what motivates me to stay healthy and active. I like to switch up my routine so no two days are ever the same. I alternate between spinning class at Soul Cycle, circuits at Barry’s Bootcamp and Training Mate, and reformer pliates. When I’m traveling, a hotel gym or run on the beach is enough! So back to my motivation. Well, health and summer bikinis are two top reasons, but I have to confess that cute workout wear is also a factor in getting me to the gym! Today’s activewear is playful, detailed, colorful, and super flattering. I’ve been loving pieces from Splits 59, Lorna Jane, and New Balance. It’s a very ‘LA’ trend to wear workout clothes to run errands and meet people for coffee (long before or after you’re actually sweating it out), and in these fun pieces, I kind of like the idea. Check out my top picks hitting the athleisure trend head on. Being active isn’t just about looking cute; a 2012 study found that by age 14 girls are dropping out of sports at two times the rate of boys – because of self esteem and body image problems. That makes me really sad, because exercise is one of the biggest stress busters, and one of the most fun activities I do. I need it for mental clarity and time to switch off, just as much as the physical side. That’s why I’m so excited to share with you This Girl Can – an empowering UK initiative to get women moving and sweating no matter how well they do, how they look, or what level they start at (check out the video for some serious motivation). Find a new class with a friend (i just tried the Barre, which was awesome), join a gym, or simply start taking the stairs – lets get physical ladies!

 xx,  “Dear Louise, I’m not used to wearing heels but when I try to wear them to look more stylish, my feet hurt so much after only 3 steps I can’t even walk. How can I get used to them? Thanks a lot.” – Yara Wearing high heels is definitely something you need to practice before you feel fully comfortable but, I know a few easy tricks that make it easier. Focus on finding a more stable heel – chunky, stacked, and platform styles are all great options for beginners. And while you may be tempted to start with sky-high pumps, look for something comfortable and walkable with a heel of about 2 inches, you’d be surprised how much height you still get. I’d love to wear it but I have no idea which colour goes well with red. (I don’t like black and red together). Could you please help me?” –Clotilde I don’t really like black and red together either! Instead, pair your red skirt with a warm neutral, like caramel, taupe or cream. Depending on the tone of red, if it has hints of orange in it, try pairing it with olive green too. Stick to a soft, thin fabric like cashmere or cotton – a simpe tee, a cropped sweater or a crisp white shirt, are great options. Don’t mix in too many other colors with the red, let the skirt be the star of the show.    xx,    Ahh, the trench coat. While it seems like embellished jackets, tailored blazers and cool-girl anoraks get all of the fun, there’s a reason this unsung hero of classic styling has a prominent place on every streetstyle pinterest board ever. It’s timeless, and always chic. It doesn’t matter what age you are, or what kind of style you like, everyone needs one. A trench can be retro with flares; urban with white tennis shoes, sexy with a leather dress and heels – it really works for everybody. I wore this thin, taupe Reiss version out in London last week, when I was in town for the launch party of Front Roe. I keep the outfit underneath simple (black leather skinnies and my favorite black tee from Anine Bing), but added major heels and a little shine with stacked gold accessories. I finished it off with messy waves and a bronzed smokey eye to make the overall look a little edgier, perfect for transitioning your outfit from day to night.

  Photography by Jessica Weber When it comes to tonal layering, breaking the so-called “rules” of fashion never looked so good. Simple yet sophisticated, tonal outfit combinations are attention-grabbing and, once you look at your closet with fresh eyes, easy to pull together with what you already own. The trick? Pick a color that works for you and then add lighter and darker tones of that same hue, with outer layers and accessories. I also like to throw a metallic or a neutral into the mix, for balance. In this particular NYFW outfit, I opted for contrasting fabrics to give the look a modern and interesting finish. For a little extra personality, I added a studded, tasseled clutch (this Rebecca Minkoff one is so fun!). What do you guys think? How would you rock a tonal look?        Get The Look: Vest by Marissa Webb (similar style here), Sweater by Reiss, Shoes by Jimmy Choo, Jewelry by Baublebar, Jean by Sass and Bide, Bag by Rebecca Minkoff       xx,  photography by Ryan Chua Hi guys, A few weeks ago, I shared some of my personal story in response to your questions about my career in fashion, and I was completely touched by your comments! I feel incredibly lucky to do what I do for a living, especially coming off an exciting week – from NYFW to the Oscars. I am honored to be featured in Forbes Magazine today, talking about my career. So in honor of all that – I put together my top tips for how to break into a career in the fashion industry. I hope it inspires you to chase after your personal and professional goals. Work Experience Once you’re 18, start building up as much work experience as possible. Even if you don’t enjoy something, it’s not a waste of time – it will help direct you towards what you do want to do. Also ask for people’s cards when you leave, and follow up with an email the next day. Being grateful and keeping in touch means you’ll be top of mind when an internship or role comes available. Ask Favors Ask for advice. When you’re still at school or college, talk to the careers advisor but also ask friend’s parents who might work in the industry you’re keen on, for tips. One of my friend’s dads at school helped set up work experience for me at a news station in London. It was such a cool experience, and it never would have happened if I hadn’t asked that favor. Don’t be shy. Internships Be prepared to get a second job or to couch-surf as an intern: they are very rarely paid, but the experience is second-to-none. Keep smiling, your upbeat nature will get you noticed. Get in early, stay late and be nice to the other interns – all your behavior will be noted, even when you think you’re being ignored! Your Resume Bosses or assistants spend an average of 8 seconds scanning a resume before making a decision. So make yours short and sweet. Tweak your resume for every person you send it to. And by that, I don’t mean lie! I mean – research the company you’re sending it to and see what they are most interested in. Is it a lifestyle magazine specializing in travel? Then your ‘interest’s section should be more travel focused. Never put a photo on your CV, that’s cheesy. Keep it to one page maximum, and have the name of prior employers or schools in bold, centered, so the important information is clear. Some employers are old school and like a hard copy – so I would mail your resume and cover letter, as well as emailing it. A week later, follow up with a polite phone call. How to Interview During an interview, don’t forget to express that you would love to work there. People often leave without showing keenness, which can come across lackluster. Doyour homework – know about every section of the magazine or every segment of the TV show or every runway show the designer has ever done. Ask one or two thoughtful questions. Knowing how to dress and what to say during an interview is key. I’ve written an entire chapter about this in my book, FrontRoe, so pick up a copy here!
  Styling and Fashion Writing You don’t necessarily need a vocational diploma, but it helps to combine a course or degree with practical work experience. Be proactive: start a blog, shoot your own editorials with friends. It’s not about having tonnes of cash to create something, but more can you show off an innovative approach? Fresh creativity? A witty or captivating point of view? Even if you can’t wangle your way into a fashion show, check out the images online and write your own reviews. Editors and stylists want to see a spark of something determined and unique about you. Don’t Bite the Hand that Feeds You It’s natural to want to progress in your career, but if you’re going to look for other jobs whilst at one, do it from a personal email address and be respectful of your current employees. The fashion and TV industries are way too tiny: people talk and people tend to know exactly what’s going on. And on that note, steer clear of office politics. Gossip and grapevines will always exist, but they’re a back-firing, downward spiral and you’re better off rising above the tittle-tattle. No Short-Cuts The major influencers in the fashion community have worked hard for many years to get where they belong. Overnight success usually takes about a decade in the making. Even Ryan Seacrest started out doing just radio for years before he hit the screen. I’m sure you guys realize this, but hard work means long hours and way more unglamorous moments than fabulous ones…. Not that I’m trying to put you off – the golden moments are 100 times worth waiting for! Never Give Up The route to success is nothing like a straight line. There will be bumps in the road and knock backs, but learn from them, and let yourself become stronger as a result. I must have sent out 30 letters for my first internship, but I knew I only needed one reply saying yes, to kick off my career. You might not get the promotion, someone might be rude to you, your shoot or piece might get spiked (an old fashioned term for dumped!) but remember – nobody wanted to buy Alexander McQueen’s pieces until Isabella Blow bought the lot. Olivier Rousteng, Balenciaga’s creative director, waited on the steps at Milk Studios every morning for weeks until someone gave him an internship there. Be Nice I went to a fashion awards ceremony in LA last night and three speakers – Nicole Richie, Miley Cyrus and Gigi Hadid – all preached the virtues of being ‘nice’ to each other in this industry. What a better environment to create, to welcome newbies into the fold, to be kind to your peers, to appreciate a mentor and have the potential to become one yourself? Never underestimate the impact of being nice in the workplace. xx,    photography by Ryan Chua I’ve never been the girl who considers black her go-to closet color: I love brights, I love prints, I love colors and fun on my clothes and accessories. But this winter, I’ve definitely been going through a darker phase. Although black is unequivocally a beloved color in the fashion world, wearing it head to toe is totally new to me. Don’t tell my wardrobe, but I have to admit I’m loving it! The way I’ve adopted black into my own wardrobe while staying true to my lighter spirited, mix and match style has been to blend textures. With this look, worn last week at NYFW, I mixed pebbled and patent leather, and solid and polka dot printed fabrics. The look was definitely as chic as allover black, but a little more me. A few modern Audrey Hepburn-inspired accents like oversize sunnies and pearl earrings, and I was set, my love of minimal black styling cemented in my style repertoire. You finally feel like an adult, but your style still could read unsure about where you fit in – finishing college, just starting a job or internship – or full-blown working woman. This is a time in your life where you should embrace trying new styles, finding what works for you, and investing in staples that will remain in your closet for years to come. I’m an apple shape. What can I do to make me look and feel sexy again but embracing the curves?” – Carli  Figuring out your body shape and learning how to dress it is the key to finding your most flattering style. So you’re already halfway there, knowing that you’re an apple shape. Apples hold most of their weight around their middle. So the minute you put on weight, it goes to your tummy, back or boobs (not your bottom of hips, like a pair shape). Apples usually have great legs and slimmer arms, so that’s where we need to focus attention. Skinny jeans or leather leggings worn with cute heels will really show off your lower half, whilst you can flatter your middle by layering a printed scarf (a small pattern and a light weight, lofty fabric is best, check out Theodora & Callum), over a looser, dark colored top with 3/4 sleeves. Don’t make the mistake of wearing low slung hipster jeans, these can lead to muffin tops. Instead, structure your waist with a slightly higher rise jean.   xx,    Guys, we’re almost two weeks into 2015– how are your New Year’s resolutions holding up? I made a few, and I’ve realized, that like lots of things in my life, they’ve got a lot to do with fashion! So why not add some more, style commandments let’s call them, and make this year our best dressed yet? Here a handful of guides and insights (many more of which are in my book!) I’ve come to know after living, writing and breathing the fashion world. How To Wear Print Print is everywhere! But a lot for the ladies I work with on makeovers, are a little afraid to wear it. It’s not a ‘hide in the corner’ kinda look, you will definitely turn heads in a bright print, so amp up your confidence, and go for it. Opt for a small, finer pattern which creates a clever optical illusion of an even surface, hiding lumps and bumps. Conversely, avoid giant flowers or shapes, as they draw the eye to one or two areas on the body, which is less flattering. Use print to attract and deflect attention on certain areas of your figure. So for example if you are pear shaped, rock some darker denim or black trousers on your lower half, and draw the eye up with a printed top or scarf. Ignore the old rule that horizontal stripes add bulk to your figure. A chic little Breton top with thin navy stripes, is very flattering on everybody. Use vertical lines and prints with panels to your advantage too: dark panels down the sides of a fitted dress or skirt, shaves off pounds and really slims down the torso and hips.   How To Choose The Right Accessories Accessories are the best, and cheapest way to revamp your existing wardrobe. They’re also a means by which to have fun with fashion: to channel some of the more daring runway trends that you wouldn’t perhaps want to sport head-to-toe. First, do your research. I like to flick through magazines, my favorite bloggers’ Instagram accounts, and check out the runway shows online. Which colors, textures or new shape of shoe and bag really catch my eye? And how would they work with my existing wardrobe? Once you’ve decided that, say, a blush clutch and patent sandals with a bejeweled block heel is what you’re after (these are both on my list right now!), then start shopping. Sometimes the rabbit warren of similar alternatives can turn out great, and you end up with something totally different, but fabulous. Don’t be afraid to wear a hat, either. This is the one item I have trouble getting my makeover subjects to wear, because hats aren’t part of our everyday uniform like they used to be mid-20th century and before. However, a cool felt Fedora or oversized beanie in Winter, or a cream Panama or leather visor in Summer, can make an average outfit into a chic, eye-catching one.   How To Dress Yourself Slimmer Wear something fitted and structured. Contrary to popular belief, hiding under a baggy muumuu will actually make you look bigger. Be aware of texture too. Ruffles and pleats add bulk, and silky tops embellished with big gemstones, sequins or beads, can also add to your shape. Instead, a supportive fabric that hugs, but doesn’t cling, will work wonders. Choose the right underwear too, this makes a massive difference. Supportive underpants and the right size bra will change your silhouette beyond belief – and the old Bridget Jones knickers have come a long way, some designs are now super sexy! Wear heels, and walk like a ballerina. Even if you find high heels tricky, a little kitten heel or a wedge will streamline your figure, and make your clothes look surprisingly better. Good posture, instead of slouching or hunching, can shave up to 10 pounds off your figure. 10 pounds! Now that’s worth standing up straight for!   How To Shop The Sales Check your closet before you leave, to figure out exactly what you do and don’t need. That way, you won’t come back with another pair of identical ankle boots. Before buying a new piece, imagine how you’d wear it three different ways with pieces you already own. Sales buys are often a snap judgement, so you want to make sure this piece fits into the rest of your wardrobe. Stick to your size. Being tempted to squeeze your toes into that very last pair of Jimmy Choo pumps, even though they’re a size too small, means you’ll only stare forlornly at them all year, or wear them once and come home with never-again blisters. Get up early, to avoid the crowds as much as possible. Even though sales can be a stressful, busy atmosphere, still try pieces on if you can. Most sales items are non returnable, so getting your new velvet dress home before realizing it doesn’t fit, sucks. Invest in luxury classics. The sales are a great time to buy a piece of discounted cashmere, a leather pencil skirt, or a great camel coat. These pieces, no matter the season, won’t date. Opt for the elegant, high quality and tailored pieces, instead of the more daring, on-trend pieces that won’t be on-trend for much longer. What other style tips are you dying to learn? Leave a comment or ask me a question– I’m here to help you make 2015 your most stylish year. xx,  I recently shot a fun post with The Coveteur where I shared my tips and tricks for getting all dressed up in Winter weather. One particular outfit really charmed me – it was an all white get-up that I paired with two of my favorite style tricks: a classic camel coat, and a pop-bright pair of heels. These two outfit elements are brilliant must-haves in every girl’s closet. A camel coat is undoubtedly one of the most sophisticated, versatile pieces you can own. It’s a versatile staple, I wear mine to elevate anything from jeans and a tee to a feminine evening dress. It’s so timeless, you’ll wear it now and forever, so if you can, invest is a well-tailored, good quality one. You’ll be collecting compliments on it for a long time! A foil to this classic closet staple? Bright shoes. They’re the perfect way to liven up a neutral palette, and you’ll find yourself matching a pair of bold, colorful strappy sandals with any outfit, day or night.           Get The Look: Sweater by J.McLaughlin (similar style here), Blouse by J.McLaughlin (similar style here), Trousers by J.McLaughlin (similar style here), Clutch by J.McLaughlin (similar style here),    xx,  As far as outfit-making footwear goes this season, nothing looks as good as a stylish pair of over-the-knee boots. Thigh-highs are a key player in every fall to winter wardrobe.        What is your best outfit for a romantic dinner with your boyfriend? Hair by Kelsey Deuel, Makeup by Suzie K, Styling by Louise Roe, Art Direction by Michelle Aquino. How do I rush around the city all day, still looking cute without sacrificing comfort? That’s one of the most popular questions I get asked by you guys, and it’s often a tricky one to get just right. The weather might be freezing, the streets wet and cobbled (like London – a disaster for new heels) and if, like me, you’re hopping on and off the bus or tube all day, it’s a bit too urban to rock your glitziest sequins and faux fur. But you still want to look chic, right?! Let me talk you through one of my favorite days from the holidays just gone, and how I put my look together. One of the things I love best about visiting London is that I get to play tourist in my old city. I started my day with breakfast in the Mirror room at the Rosewood Hotel – which is honestly my favorite pitstop in town: the décor is elegant but cozy, the food is yummy, and the building, which has an enormous courtyard surrounded by Victorian architecture, is awe-inspiring. So after kicking off a long day’s sight-seeing with eggs benedict and two lattes, I checked out the new Horst fashion photography exhibit at the V&A, the sales (of course! Too much spent at Tops​hop!) lunch at the French Brasserie Colbert on Sloane Square, and then the Charles Dickens museum (his old townhouse, in fact) something I’d always wanted to do since I studied him fanatically at uni. Planning an outfit for a long day on your feet is always tricky: I didn’t want feet full of blisters and a freezing red nose by 4pm. So these kitten heel boots were perfect for walking non-stop; and layering a thin suede jacket under another thin overcoat was great for constantly switching between indoors and outdoors. The tassles? Well they were there for nothing but fun! I’ve never, ever worn tassels before and when I saw the fun suede jacket and to-die-for bucket J&M Davidson drawstring bag, I knew they had to take an outing together. Why not bring a little cowgirl chic to the mean streets of London?         Get The Look: Coat by Marks and Spencer (similar style here), Boots by Kurt Geiger, Bag by Monique Davidson (similar style here), Tassle Jacket by Haute Hippie, Jeans by Frame denim, Sweater by Marilyn Monroe, Bracelet by Monica Vinader, Bangle by Gorjana, Watch by Chopard (similar style here), and Ring by Claire Aristides   xx,  “Is it ok to wear gold and silver jewelry together?” – Lindsay Absolutely! Mixing your metals is a huge trend – I like to throw some rose gold in there too for a pretty trio. My tip? Make sure there’s an equal balance of colors across your fingers, wrist and necklace, so it feels proportioned. xx,    I’ve grown to love January 1st. It used to be a slightly depressing day – full of headaches from partying the night before, and feeling bummed out about going right back to school or work – until, a couple of years ago, I decided to embrace it as a positively exciting, fresh start. My Dad always says, you start the next day, the night before – and it’s so true. So I want to kick off the New Year, the night before -not only with a big party – but with some New Year resolutions that I’ll actually stick to! This year, I’m making three sets of resolutions: lifestyle resolutions, beauty resolutions, and fashion resolutions. So here’s the lifestyle one: enjoy the actual moment, not the photo opp. On a recent trip to San Francisco, I spent the weekend looking up. By that I mean, taking in the beautiful architecture, cycling over the Golden Gate Bridge, visiting an art gallery or two, and soaking up the city’s history. In LA, where I live, the daily grind means I rush from errand to errand, from meeting to meeting, with my head down, and without stopping much to smell the roses much. I realized that I spend far too much time stressing out over work schedules, or worse – staring into my phone – rather than looking up and enjoying the amazing city and landscape surrounding me. Being mindful is something I am reading more about, and the books I’ve enjoyed include Mindful London, and Wherever You Go, There You Are. Second up, let’s talk fashion. These are simple. I never wear black, because I prefer the expressive nature of bright colors, but I’m going to wear more fabulous black shoes. They convey such an elegance, and I kicked off my newfound habit with a spree at Bluefly. I got these patent pointy Jimmy Choos, suede black Charlotte Olympia platforms with a gold sole, and lower black heels with a gold strip across the toe from Giuseppe Zanotti. (And yes, I realize I just made a New Year’s Resolution out of doing more shoe shopping. But hey, it’s all in the name of research!). I resolve to wear more lace: there’s barely any of it in my wardrobe, and I love the fabric. Same goes for pencil skirts. On my days off or nights out when I’m not filming, I’ll often take the easy option and throw on some high waisted jeans. Time to make more effort and try some of the leather pencil skirts looking so sexy in the boutique windows right now. My beauty New Year’s resolutions are to learn how to blow out my hair. I haven’t got a clue; I’m useless with a curling iron! I’m determined to master a beachy wave. So I’m hitting my friends’ new salon Striiike, owned by three gorgeous sisters in the industry (one’s a hair stylist, one’s a makeup artist and the other an eyebrow threader) to make friends with my curling iron. Oh and finally, I need to wear bolder and brighter lipstick, more often. Your body is your body and it’s time to embrace it, none of us has the perfect, dream figure and yet you can alter its silhouette by dressing the right way. Being pear-shaped means you carry most of your weight around your hips and thighs. I recommend drawing the attention upwards to your slimmer waist and decolletage. Darker trousers worn with a small heel will elongate and flatter the legs. Then, wear bright colors in more fitted fabrics on your top half, especially those with a nice wide boat neck or round neckline. Anything off the shoulder for evening will also look fab. xx,                    Get The Look: Sweater by Piperlime, Skirt by Cameo the label (similar here), Heels by Jimmy Choo, Vintage Jewelry from Chairish (similar here and here) xx,    There are a few ways to do holiday party style. I’ve seen the peplum skirt, or the cropped mermaid as I like to call it, on the red carpet at major events like the Oscars, where stars wear long, floor-skimming gowns in the very same shape. So to see it on the runways this season on everyday skirts, is really cool. Translating the silhouette from a formal, sparkly gown, into a more relaxed street style look, is fun. Basically, it’s a slim line of a skirt that flares out with a ruffle just below your knees. Wear yours with a heel: the extra height of keeps the volume from being overwhelming, and streamlines your legs. I absolutely don’t know what to wear on this occasion, I want to have a smart look without being too boring! Can you help me? Merci!” — Phae Looking killer for an important work event is really important, you need to strike the balance between being formal but also edgy with your fashion: we can do much better than a boring black suit! I’m a huge fan of high-waisted pencil skirts that stop just below the knee. Go for a muted print, such as herringbone or checks. A thin, mock-turtle-neck sweater (it’s shorter than a turtle neck, the fabric doesn’t fold over) in dark color such as burgundy or purple, tucked in, will look really elegant. Finished off with pointy stiletto pumps (avoid a platform – a thin sole looks more ladylike), and a small bag which matches either your top, skirt or shoes. The smaller details count too: paint your nails a simple neutral, and make sure your hair is neatly blown out or tied back with a combed center-part. The temps have cooled down enough to allow for me to whip out these killer leather joggers, and I couldn’t be more excited. They sound like a bold fashion statement, but they’re surprisingly versatile. I paired them with a bold (yet breezy) button down and of-the-moment mules, and I can imagine a whole slew of other outfits. This closet classic has always lent a little polish to any outfit it’s a part of, and this season it’s the perfect addition to more casual looks. Try rolling the sleeves a little, half tucking the hem into jeans, or even styling with overalls (which I’ve recently discovered are super wearable). Another trick I love is adding an accessory: either buttoning your blouse all the way up and sliding a statement necklace under the collar, or keeping it loose and open with a pair of shades as your necklace-replacement. Even if you have no intention of wearing the sunglasses, they make for a great accessory.

That means no chipped nail polish, hair clean and blown-out, and a little makeup. Closed-toe heels are more formal than open sandals, and keep your clothes fitted, but not too tight or short. Check out the style of the boutique’s clothing, and try to dress in the same manner. You want your clothes to show off your personality, but also to prove you’d fit in really well at the company. Avoid jeans, but try cropped pants, a knee-length a-line skirt or a printed dress. And take your boldest, brightest small handbag (with a strap, don’t take a clutch) – that will show off your personal style and hopefully become a talking point. xx,  This outfit rocks – I had so much fun putting it together! I thought about a boot or a pointy flat shoe, but these awesome chunky-heel sandals were perfect – for once, I’m not adding a pop of color to my look! What do you guys think? Little changes, like adding more delicate accessories and pumping up the volume on beauty, are easy ways to update an outfit. How would you guys style this outfit? The right underwear can completely change your silhouette, your posture, and your confidence levels. Every woman should own a t-shirt bra, in a nude tone. These are great for wearing day-to-day, and super comfy. The cups are molded and seam-free, creating a flattering shape that won’t snag or look bumpy under thin tops. A nude bra will disappear better than white will, under a white tee; just try to find a nude shade closest to your skin tone. xx,              Get the look: Jeans by Paper Denim Cloth, Tee by Uniqlo (similar style here), Blazer by Reiss, Handbag by Kurt Geiger (similar style here), Watch by Daniel Wellington, Shoes from Dune (similar style here), Sunglasses by Vow London (similar styles here)   xx,  When it comes to style staples, roll-neck sweaters are definitely one of mine! Unlike many Fall seasons, when there’s a ton of black and grey out there, this year is all about mixing your jeans or black pants with major brights. Fuschia, orange, emerald green, mustard, and scarlet are all going to be huge. My tip? Invest in a bright cape or coat, it’ll make a great first impression when you walk into that interview or dinner date. xx,  I adore colors and prints — they liven up everything, including grey days in NYC. Burnt orange is one of my favorite colors for fall, and I love how this print highlights it. Clover Canyon makes incredible modern patterns out of florals and nature prints, but the effect is always futuristic thanks to the geometry of the pieces. This set was made out of neoprene, a great structural material that made the silhouette of this look both ladylike and edgy.
